## Migration gate note — recorded, NOT decided (applies to rows 22–23 and 28, 30, 32, 34, 36, 38, 40)

Every `$n.migrate` row above, and the `onPreMigrate`/`onPostMigrate` callbacks, rest on a
STATE `data` gate: "existing database at an older version" (app upgrade, not fresh install).
That gate is what makes these reachable despite the fresh-install default: the trigger is
a real, in-app database version, not a hypothetical external condition. **If the project
rule is strictly fresh-install-only, those `migrate` rows and rows 22–23 flip together to
UNREACHABLE** (kind to be decided at merge; the internal chains themselves are printed,
so this is a gate decision, not a missing-caller decision). The `$n.<init>` rows do NOT
flip on that rule: they run at `DatabaseMigration.<clinit>` whenever `getMigrations`
builds the array, fresh install included. Do not merge the `migrate` rows without
resolving this gate question. This file does NOT decide it.

## Migration gate note — recorded, NOT decided (applies to rows 2–10)

`DatabaseMigration.java` `RoomDatabaseMigration` is `Migration(4, 5)` (freshly confirmed
:131-136, `super(4, 5)`). Room invokes `migrate()` only when opening a database whose
stored version is 4 (stepping 4→5 toward the current version 9, `AppDatabase.java:51`).
On a **fresh install** Room creates version 9 directly and `migrate()` never runs.
The verdicts for rows 2–10 therefore rest on a STATE `data` gate — "a pre-existing
version-4 database is present at first DB open" — with Room dispatch to `migrate`
INFERRED (Room source not printed). **If the project rule is strictly fresh-install-only,
rows 2–10 flip together to UNREACHABLE** (kind to be decided at merge; the internal
chain itself is printed, so this is a gate decision, not a missing-caller decision).
Row 1 (`<init>`) does NOT flip: it runs whenever `getMigrations` builds the array,
fresh install included. Do not merge rows 2–10 without resolving this gate question.
This file does NOT decide it. The same gate question applies to batch 48's `$n.migrate`
rows; the two batches must be decided consistently.

## Table

`confirmed_by`: SOURCE = a direct app-to-app call site naming the method is printed in
app source (including a printed internal call site where the calling method is itself
SOURCE-anchored); INFERRED = the method is reached only through unprinted
framework/library dispatch (Room invoking a registered `Migration`, JVM class
initialisation, javac anonymous-class construction) where the registration or first use
is printed. This is the per-row convention, not the whole-route convention used in some
earlier batches; labels are unaffected, only `confirmed_by` would change at merge if the
merger adopts the whole-route convention.

## Why every row is OPEN (not UNREACHABLE proposed, not REACHABLE proposed)

1. **Generated source is absent from the upload.** Every row is a generated `*Binding` static (`bind`/`inflate`). The generated `*Binding`, `*BindingImpl`, and `databinding.DataBinderMapperImpl` sources are not in the tree, so no definition and no generated dispatch step can be printed from them in this pass.
2. **Direct-call absence does not close a generated row.** The freshly fetched saved grep proves **no direct app-code call** to any batch 5–7 `bind`/`inflate` static in the app Java tree, and its positive controls prove the grep was not empty (DataBindingUtil.inflate in 30 files; setContentView in the three activities above). That proves app code goes through `DataBindingUtil`. It does NOT prove generated code never calls these statics, and a grep over app source cannot rule out generated dispatch.
3. **The flip to UNREACHABLE rests on an unprinted premise.** It requires the premise that generated mapper code constructs binding objects directly (via constructors / `getDataBinder`) and never routes through the base `bind`/`inflate` statics. That premise is not printed in the grep file, in any audit, or in either layout fetched here. The batch-10 audit applied exactly this reasoning to identical-shape rows (`ItemTorrentDownloadableFileBinding` / `ItemTorrentListBinding` / `ItemTrackersListBinding` / `TagsListItemBinding`) and left them OPEN ("DataBindingUtil request exists; generated bind dispatch absent" → OPEN). Flipping batches 5–7 while batch 10 stays OPEN on the same ground would bake in the inconsistency the batches 1–12 verification flags for merge. The merger must reconcile that inconsistency project-wide, not in these batch files.
4. **Task rule.** Never flip OPEN to UNREACHABLE without a saved full-repo grep. No saved full-repo grep was run in this pass, and the saved grep above is an app-tree grep, not a full-repo grep for generated code (generated code is absent from the tree it searched). Therefore no row is proposed UNREACHABLE, even though the plan says UNREACHABLE and the direct-call grep points that way.
5. **No row is REACHABLE either.** No printed direct call names any row's static, and no printed generated dispatch names any row's static. A `DataBindingUtil` request for the row's layout proves the layout is bound; it does not prove the base `bind`/`inflate` static runs, because the request goes through the mapper/Impl path whose dispatch is unprinted.
6. **Binding-variant gap (per row, undecided).** Even where a DataBindingUtil request for a layout is printed, nothing printed selects WHICH of the 6 overloads (bind vs inflate; with vs without the `Object` DataBindingComponent argument; with vs without parent/attachToParent) the framework or mapper would invoke, if it invoked a base Binding static at all. The 6 overloads of one class therefore cannot be distinguished on this evidence, and no single overload can be promoted while its siblings stay OPEN on the same evidence.
7. **Child bindings do not change this.** Two batch-5 classes have no direct DataBindingUtil site at all: `AddTorrentInfoBinding` is a child binding of the requested `fragment_add_torrent_info` layout (fresh include, above), and `DetailTorrentAppbarBinding` is a child binding of the requested `fragment_detail_torrent` layout (fresh include, above). Their creation, if any, is by generated parent-Impl code, which is unprinted. They are OPEN on the same ground as the directly requested classes, not on a weaker ground — and the include does not make them REACHABLE, because the parent Impl dispatch is unprinted.

## confirmed_by convention in this file

- `INFERRED` for every OPEN row, following the batch-2 / batch-5 merge-ready convention: at best an inference, because the generated source is absent. It does NOT mean the row is close to closed; it means the ceiling of what can be said without generated source or a saved full-repo grep covering generated code.
- No row uses `DYN`, `FW-MEMORY`, or `UNVERIFIED` as `confirmed_by`. No row is `SOURCE`: no definition or call site for any row's method was freshly printed in this pass (the audits and grep are printed evidence about the absence of direct calls and the presence of layout requests, not call sites for the rows themselves).
- Undecided conventions flagged per row (never decided here): **generated-mapper dispatch** (does the mapper/Impl ever call a base `bind`/`inflate` static?); **binding-variant** (which overload, if any); **strict-entry** (see below); **binding getViewModel** (no getter row in these batches — not applicable); **finalize** (no finalize row — not applicable); **$N mapping** (no anonymous/inner-class row — not applicable); **migration gate** (no Room migration row — not applicable).

## Strict-entry

OPEN rows assert no route, so no entry step is claimed for any row. Strict-entry does not change an OPEN verdict. It matters only if a row is later flipped: any future REACHABLE flip for a row in these batches would additionally need its launcher → host-activity / dialog-show / fragment-attach step printed fresh (none of those steps was freshly printed in this pass, except the two layout includes above, which are not entry steps). Do not apply a strict-entry revert to OPEN rows — they are already OPEN.

## Route anchors (printed, fresh)

1. **Repository construction.** `RepositoryHelper.getTorrentRepository` (:43-47) constructs
   `new TorrentRepositoryImpl(appContext, AppDatabase.getInstance(appContext))`;
   `getFeedRepository` (:52-56) constructs `new FeedRepositoryImpl(...)`;
   `getTagRepository` (:69-72) constructs `new TagRepositoryImpl(...)`.
   Printed callers, all fresh: `TorrentEngine.java:130-131`, `DetailTorrentViewModel.java:109,114`,
   `MainViewModel.java:74`, `AddTagViewModel.java:42`, `FeedViewModel.java:64`,
   `FeedItemsViewModel.java:59`, `AddFeedViewModel.java:63`, `SelectTagViewModel.java:44`,
   `FeedFetcherWorker.java:77`, `FeedDownloaderWorker.java:79`.
2. **Database / DAO access.** `AppDatabase.java` declares the abstract DAO accessors
   `torrentDao()` (:61), `fastResumeDao()` (:63), `feedDao()` (:65), `tagInfoDao()` (:67),
   registers `UriConverter` via `@TypeConverters({UriConverter.class})` (:53), and builds the
   database via `Room.databaseBuilder(...)` in `buildDatabase` (:81-85) called from
   `getInstance` (:69-74). At runtime Room returns the generated `*_Impl` for each accessor;
   that step is library-generated, hence every `*_Impl` row below is INFERRED, never SOURCE.
3. **Room-generated classes are not in the upload.** `FastResumeDao_Impl.java`,
   `FeedDao_Impl.java`, `TagInfoDao_Impl.java` and `TorrentDao_Impl.java` do not exist under
   `final_java_only/` (generated at build time from the DAO interfaces / abstract class).
   If the merger requires a printed generated file, the `_Impl` rows revert to OPEN — they
   are not SOURCE, and this file does not claim otherwise.
4. **Strict-entry caveat.** Routes anchor at ViewModels / Workers / Engine / Session /
   RepositoryHelper, not at a printed launcher entry. Under a strict launcher-entry rule all
   REACHABLE rows below revert to OPEN; the proposed counts follow the sibling-batch
   (e.g. batches 22/23, 48-50) anchor convention, not the strict rule.

## Undecided conventions recorded per row, NOT decided

- **Strict-entry:** see anchor 4 above. No launcher step was freshly printed in this pass.
- **`confirmed_by` (per-row, not whole-route):** SOURCE = a printed direct app-code call site
  naming the method (for hand-written repository methods: the printed repository body plus a
  printed ViewModel/Worker/Engine/Session caller); INFERRED = reached only through unprinted
  framework/library/generated dispatch (Room dispatch to `_Impl`, Rx subscription, JVM class
  initialisation) where the interface call / registration / first use IS printed. Under a
  whole-route INFERRED convention, SOURCE rows whose route crosses unprinted dispatch would
  read INFERRED; labels are unaffected, only `confirmed_by` would change.
- **Room `$N` mapping (convention (vii)):** for `FeedDao_Impl$N` (batch 51) and
  `TagInfoDao_Impl$N` (batch 52) this file uses the SET argument, not the individual
  assignment: every generated helper in each set is used because every underlying DAO
  operation is called on a freshly printed route (see the per-row set notes), so a swap
  within a set changes no label. The proposal lead orders (declaration order / canonical
  Room order) are recorded as leads only. The one exception is pinned, not set-assigned:
  `FeedDao_Impl$8.call` returns `FeedChannel`, and `getFeedByIdSingle` is the only Rx method
  returning a single FeedChannel. If the merger adopts the batch-53 strict rule — a `$N`
  row needs printed generated source or bytecode identifying that `$N` — then the strict-`$N`
  revert lists below apply, and the merger must reconcile that rule project-wide with
  batches 51-52 (set argument) vs batch 53 (OPEN on the same ground).
- **Room Callable `finalize()` (convention (vi)):** all `finalize()` rows stay OPEN (GC-only
  reasoning in each row). A project rule is still needed; they are NOT `no_trigger_in_app`.
- **`getRequiredConverters` treatment:** rows 50/27, 51/6, 52/11 stay OPEN (no printed call,
  Room dispatch for them not printed), following the batch-48/53 precedent. The batch 50-52
  proposals marked them REACHABLE INFERRED on a database-build assumption; if the merger
  adopts that assumption instead, those three rows flip together to REACHABLE INFERRED.
- **`<clinit>` on first-use evidence (convention (ix)):** batch-50 row 6 rests on a printed
  first use (construction at RepositoryHelper.java:46-47) plus a non-constant
  `TAG = getSimpleName()` (TorrentRepositoryImpl.java:41). Whether that evidence alone
  suffices project-wide is undecided; the row is INFERRED, not SOURCE.
- **Migration gate (convention (iii)):** does not arise in these batches (no migration rows).
- **Binding `getViewModel` (convention (v)), binding-variant config (iv), FragmentCallback
  (viii):** no such rows occur in batches 50-52.

  naming the method (for hand-written repository methods: the printed repository body plus a
  printed ViewModel/Worker/Engine/Session caller); INFERRED = reached only through unprinted
  framework/library/generated dispatch (Room dispatch to `_Impl`, Rx subscription, JVM class
  initialisation) where the interface call / registration / first use IS printed. Under a
  whole-route INFERRED convention, SOURCE rows whose route crosses unprinted dispatch would
  read INFERRED; labels are unaffected, only `confirmed_by` would change.

  `TagInfoDao_Impl$N` (batch 52) this file uses the SET argument, not the individual
  assignment: every generated helper in each set is used because every underlying DAO
  operation is called on a freshly printed route (see the per-row set notes), so a swap
  within a set changes no label. The proposal lead orders (declaration order / canonical
  Room order) are recorded as leads only. The one exception is pinned, not set-assigned:
  `FeedDao_Impl$8.call` returns `FeedChannel`, and `getFeedByIdSingle` is the only Rx method

  revert lists below apply, and the merger must reconcile that rule project-wide with
  batches 51-52 (set argument) vs batch 53 (OPEN on the same ground).

   `new TorrentRepositoryImpl(appContext, AppDatabase.getInstance(appContext))`;

   `getTagRepository` (:69-72) constructs `new TagRepositoryImpl(...)`.

   `MainViewModel.java:74`, `AddTagViewModel.java:42`, `FeedViewModel.java:64`,
   `FeedItemsViewModel.java:59`, `AddFeedViewModel.java:63`, `SelectTagViewModel.java:44`,
   `FeedFetcherWorker.java:77`, `FeedDownloaderWorker.java:79`.
2. **Database / DAO access.** `AppDatabase.java` declares the abstract DAO accessors
   `torrentDao()` (:61), `fastResumeDao()` (:63), `feedDao()` (:65), `tagInfoDao()` (:67),
   registers `UriConverter` via `@TypeConverters({UriConverter.class})` (:53), and builds the
   database via `Room.databaseBuilder(...)` in `buildDatabase` (:81-85) called from
   `getInstance` (:69-74). At runtime Room returns the generated `*_Impl` for each accessor;
   that step is library-generated, hence every `*_Impl` row below is INFERRED, never SOURCE.
3. **Room-generated classes are not in the upload.** `FastResumeDao_Impl.java`,
   `FeedDao_Impl.java`, `TagInfoDao_Impl.java` and `TorrentDao_Impl.java` do not exist under
   `final_java_only/` (generated at build time from the DAO interfaces / abstract class).
   If the merger requires a printed generated file, the `_Impl` rows revert to OPEN — they
   are not SOURCE, and this file does not claim otherwise.
4. **Strict-entry caveat.** Routes anchor at ViewModels / Workers / Engine / Session /
   RepositoryHelper, not at a printed launcher entry. Under a strict launcher-entry rule all
   REACHABLE rows below revert to OPEN; the proposed counts follow the sibling-batch
   (e.g. batches 22/23, 48-50) anchor convention, not the strict rule.

  naming the method (for hand-written repository methods: the printed repository body plus a
  printed ViewModel/Worker/Engine/Session caller); INFERRED = reached only through unprinted
  framework/library/generated dispatch (Room dispatch to `_Impl`, Rx subscription, JVM class

  whole-route INFERRED convention, SOURCE rows whose route crosses unprinted dispatch would
  read INFERRED; labels are unaffected, only `confirmed_by` would change.

  `TagInfoDao_Impl$N` (batch 52) this file uses the SET argument, not the individual
  assignment: every generated helper in each set is used because every underlying DAO

This batch is the STRICT-applied counterpart to batches 51-52, which already apply SET: under SET, the 21 rows below flip together to REACHABLE INFERRED with no further source print needed for the flip itself, because set membership is fully verified here. Under STRICT they stay OPEN. The merger must apply ONE rule project-wide; harmonizing 51-52 (already SET) with 53 (STRICT) in either direction changes 21 rows here and/or 46 rows across batches 50-52 (50: 3, 51: 25, 52: 18).

TorrentDao.java (77 lines) operations: add :40 (@Insert Torrent), update :43 (@Update Torrent), delete :46 (@Delete Torrent), getAllTorrents :49 (@Query plain List), getTorrentById :52 (@Query plain Torrent), getTorrentByIdSingle :55 (@Query Single Torrent), observeTorrentById :58 (@Query Flowable Torrent), addTags :61 (@Insert List TorrentTagInfo), deleteTagsByTorrentId :64 (@Query DELETE), replaceTags :67-70 (@Transaction concrete method calling deleteTagsByTorrentId :68 and addTags :69), addTag :73 (@Insert TorrentTagInfo), deleteTag :76 (@Delete TorrentTagInfo). Every TorrentDao operation is therefore called, directly or (for addTags and deleteTagsByTorrentId) through the concrete replaceTags transaction body, which is itself called by the repository. - Bind-adapter set $1-$5 (rows 10-24): plan bind descriptors, in $ order, are Torrent, TorrentTagInfo, Torrent, TorrentTagInfo, Torrent (multiset Torrent x3, TorrentTagInfo x2 — exactly as the prior revision note claimed; verified against the plan in this pass, no correction needed). That is 5 adapters for the 6 insert/update/delete methods, because addTags and addTag share one adapter; a per-method count of 6 would contradict the plan, which contains exactly 5 bind sets. All five adapters' operations are called, so every $1-$5 row is a SET member regardless of which $N serves which adapter. No membership error found.
- Statement set $6 (rows 25-26, <init> plus createQuery only, no bind): deleteTagsByTorrentId is the sole @Query method that is neither a plain select nor an Rx method, so it is the sole SharedSQLiteStatement candidate (same shape as batch-51 $5-$7). It is called through TorrentDao.replaceTags :68, called by TorrentRepositoryImpl.replaceTags :127. This set has size 1 and is pinned, not merely set-assigned, subject only to the SET-vs-STRICT convention question itself (no generated source pins $6 individually).
- Callable set $7-$8 (rows 27-28, 30-31, plus finalize rows 29/32): plan call descriptors both return Torrent. The sole Torrent-returning Rx methods are getTorrentByIdSingle (TorrentDao:55, repository :77) and observeTorrentById (TorrentDao:58, repository :82), both called. So every $7/$8 <init>/call row is a SET member. Count check: 5 bind adapters + 1 statement + 2 Callables = 8 helper classes = plan $1-$8 exactly; there is no missing or extra $ class in this batch.

## Strict-entry caveat (explicit)

Every REACHABLE route below is anchored at `TorrentService` → `TorrentEngine`, at a settings
fragment, or at `TorrentRepositoryImpl`. The launcher-to-service step, the fragment-attachment
steps, and the callers of the `TorrentRepositoryImpl` methods themselves were NOT freshly
printed in this pass, so under a strict launcher-entry rule all REACHABLE rows revert to OPEN.
The `TorrentDao_Impl` rows additionally rest on Room-generated dispatch (INFERRED), not on
printed generated source.

## Undecided conventions recorded per row, NOT decided

- **Room `$N` mapping (rows 10–32):** per the task note, all `TorrentDao_Impl$1–$8` rows
  stay OPEN unless `$N` identity is confirmed against generated source or bytecode.
  `TorrentDao.java` declaration order (add, update, delete, getAllTorrents, getTorrentById,
  getTorrentByIdSingle, observeTorrentById, addTags, deleteTagsByTorrentId, addTag, deleteTag)
  is recorded as a LEAD ONLY for a canonical-order mapping (as batches 51–52 inferred for
  other DAOs); it is NOT adopted here, because declaration order is not generated source or
  bytecode, and the `$7`/`$8` Single/Flowable callables plus the `$N.finalize()` rows
  (see below) cannot be pinned by order alone.
- **Room Callable `finalize()` (rows 29, 32):** GC-only reasoning — only the JVM GC can invoke
  `finalize()` on a created Callable, so these rows are OPEN, not `no_trigger_in_app`;
  a project rule is still needed (same as batches 51–52).
- **`confirmed_by` convention:** SOURCE = a printed direct app-to-app call site naming the
  method; INFERRED = reached only through unprinted framework/library/generated dispatch
  (Room dispatch, FsModule interface dispatch, JVM class initialisation) where the
  registration or dispatch code is printed. This is the per-row convention, not the
  whole-route convention used in some earlier batches; labels are unaffected, only
  `confirmed_by` would change at merge under the whole-route convention.

## Strict-entry caveat (explicit)

Every REACHABLE route below is anchored at `TorrentService` → `TorrentEngine`, at a settings
fragment, at `TorrentRepositoryImpl`, or at a ViewModel/Activity/IPFilterParser call site
freshly printed in this pass. The launcher-to-service step, the fragment-attachment steps,
and the callers of the `TorrentRepositoryImpl` methods themselves were NOT freshly printed
in this pass, so under a strict launcher-entry rule all REACHABLE rows revert to OPEN.
Rows 37–39 additionally require the SAF-path gate (STATE data: the URI is a SAF path, so the
resolver's L43 branch selects the SAF module); on a file-system URI the same facade calls run
the `DefaultFsModule` branch instead (batch 53).

## Undecided conventions recorded per row, NOT decided

- **`confirmed_by` convention:** SOURCE = a printed direct app-to-app call site naming the
  method (including a printed internal call from a reachable method in the same class, as
  rows 24 and 29); INFERRED = reached only through unprinted framework/library/generated
  dispatch (FsModule interface dispatch, JVM class initialisation, try-with-resources
  compiler dispatch) where the registration or dispatch code is printed. This is the
  per-row convention, not the whole-route convention used in some earlier batches; labels
  are unaffected, only `confirmed_by` would change at merge under the whole-route convention.
  Note: batch 55's proposal used SOURCE for SafFsModule rows reached via the same facade
  calls; this file keeps Agent 16's INFERRED for the analogous `SafFileSystem` rows (37–40)
  — the merger must pick one convention for module-dispatch rows across batches 53–55.
- **`buildValidFatFilename` group (rows 9, 27, 32) — RESOLVED in open-close pass 2026-11-01:**
  rows 9, 27, 32 are now REACHABLE SOURCE on fresh printed callers (see "Flips in this
  open-close pass"). The batch-4 graph claim for `isValidFatFilename` is a different method
  and was not relied on for these flips; batch-4 row 15 is unchanged (see revision note).

## Shared route / dispatch (applies to most rows below)

1. **Service anchor (from prior batches, not freshly re-printed here).**
   `TorrentService.onCreate` gets `TorrentEngine.getInstance`;
   `TorrentEngine.<init>` (local copy) calls `SystemFacadeHelper.getFileSystemFacade(appContext)`
   (:132) and `SystemFacadeHelper.getSystemFacade(appContext)` (:138).
2. **Facade dispatch (printed in the Agent 17 pass).**
   `SystemFacadeHelper.getFileSystemFacade` (:39–46) builds `FileSystemFacadeImpl` with
   `FsModuleResolverImpl` (:42–43). `FsModuleResolverImpl.resolveFsByUri` (:42–49): if
   `Utils.isSafPath(appContext, uri)` return the `SafFsModule` (constructed :37); else if
   file-system path return the default module; else throw `UnknownUriException`.
   `Utils.isSafPath` (local copy :657–658) is `SafFileSystem.getInstance(appContext).isSafPath(path)`.
3. **SAF-branch condition.** Every `SafFsModule`/`SafFileSystem` row below runs only when the
   uri passed through the facade is a SAF (content-scheme) uri, so the resolver picks
   `safModule`. That is a data condition on the caller's uri (e.g. a user-chosen SAF
   download dir), not a separate gate in the graph sense; it is noted on each row as
   "SAF branch". The interface dispatch `FileSystemFacadeImpl` → `FsModule` is printed code,
   so the Agent 17 proposal marked SafFsModule rows SOURCE; this file RETAINS that
   convention for batch 55 (see "Undecided conventions") — it differs from batches 53–54,
   where the analogous module-dispatch rows are INFERRED, and the merger must pick one
   convention across batches 53–55. Labels are unaffected by that choice.

## Undecided conventions recorded, not decided

- **Strict-entry:** routes anchor at DetailTorrentFragment, AddTorrentViewModel, and TorrentFilterCollection
  call sites, not at a printed launcher entry. Under a strict launcher-entry rule, all 22 REACHABLE
  rows revert to OPEN. Merger must pick one strict-entry convention for all batches.
- **Confirmed_by:** per-row convention used here — SOURCE = a printed direct app-code call site naming
  the method (including internal calls from a SOURCE-anchored method); INFERRED = JVM/generated/framework
  dispatch where registration or first use is printed. Labels are unaffected; only `confirmed_by`
  would change at merge under a whole-route convention.
- **Gated OPEN rows (rows 2, 3, 14):** OPEN is a condition-satisfaction statement, not a kind.
  A grep finding a caller with `removeDirectoryIndex` non-empty, `removeAuthentication=false`, or
  `decode=true` flips the corresponding row(s) to REACHABLE; finding none would be needed before
  any `closed_by_configuration` / `branch_never_taken` claim, and no such claim is made here.

## Generated-binding convention (recorded, NOT decided)

Generated data-binding classes (`*Binding`, `*BindingImpl`) are NOT in `final_java_only`
(build-generated), so every Impl verdict is INFERRED, never SOURCE. This file follows the
batches 67-68 convention, aligned with the prior batch-58 proposal, and records the conflict
explicitly for the merger:
- Creation/rebind-flow methods are INFERRED REACHABLE once the binding is created by a printed
  `DataBindingUtil.setContentView` call (generated ctor calls `invalidateAll()`, the framework
  rebind flow then calls `executeBindings()`).
- Variable/observable-specific methods are OPEN unless a printed use exists: `hasPendingBindings`
  (no printed caller), `onFieldChange` / `setVariable` (no printed observable registration or
  variable assignment), and a generated getter such as `getViewModel` (no printed getter call —
  a printed *setter* call is not a getter call, and generated `executeBindings` reads the field
  directly, not via the getter).
- **CONVENTION CONFLICT, deliberately NOT decided here (batch 58 row 37):** batches 65-66 marked
  binding `getViewModel` getters REACHABLE via the generated `executeBindings` read path, while
  batches 58 (this file), 63-64 (Agent 21), and 59-60 (abstract `getViewModel`) left them OPEN
  because `executeBindings` reads the `mViewModel` field, not the getter. Row 37 below is therefore
  OPEN under the 58/63-64 convention and would flip to REACHABLE INFERRED under the 65-66 convention.
  The merger must pick ONE convention for all binding getters; do not mix them batch by batch.
  The same conflict does not affect rows 32/34/35, which are OPEN under both conventions on this
  evidence (no printed caller, no printed observable use, no printed variable assignment).

## Judging rule used in this file (conventions recorded, NOT decided)
All rows are Android data-binding generated classes. Their bodies and
libretorrent.DataBinderMapperImpl are NOT in final_java_only, so no row here is SOURCE:
every REACHABLE row is INFERRED on a printed inflate/setContentView + setViewModel site
plus a printed layout, with generated mapper/Impl dispatch unprinted.
- Abstract Binding.<init>: REACHABLE INFERRED — runs as the super-call of the Impl ctor.
- Impl <clinit> / <init>(2-arg) / <init>(3-arg): REACHABLE INFERRED — class init on first
  use; the 2-arg Impl ctor is what the generated mapper returns for the printed layout,
  and the generated 2-arg ctor delegates to the 3-arg ctor.
- Impl executeBindings: REACHABLE INFERRED — generated lifecycle callback for the binding
  created at the printed inflate site (wave convention, applied uniformly even to the
  appbar Impl in batch 60; see batch-60 rows 11-12 for where that convention stops).
- Impl setViewModel / setVariable: REACHABLE INFERRED where the printed site sets the
  viewModel AND the layout declares the viewModel variable with @{...} expressions.
- Impl onFieldChange: REACHABLE INFERRED where the printed site sets the viewModel AND
  the layout binds an observable of that viewModel.
- Named Impl onChangeViewModel*: REACHABLE INFERRED where the printed site sets the
  viewModel, the layout binds the named observable, and a change to that observable is
  printed (user interaction with a two-way-bound view, an event handler, or an async
  callback). Batch-59 row 29 is flagged weak on this rule (no info-set site printed).
- Impl hasPendingBindings / invalidateAll: OPEN — no printed caller anywhere in this pass.
- Abstract Binding.getViewModel: OPEN — no printed caller. Convention (v), recorded not
  decided: this file follows batches 58/63/64 (generated executeBindings reads the
  mViewModel field, not the getter). Batches 62/65 and the batch-61 prior proposal use
  the opposite convention (getter read by executeBindings); under that convention the
  getViewModel rows in batches 59-61 flip together to REACHABLE INFERRED. The merger
  must pick ONE convention for all binding getters (including batch 80 row 23 and
  batch 84 row 40, same family).
- Anonymous Impl $N.<init> / $N.onChange: OPEN — Impl bodies unprinted, so no $N row
  can be assigned to a printed observable or change event. Convention recorded not
  decided: this file follows batches 62-64 ($N OPEN). The batch-59/60 prior proposals
  used the opposite convention ($N REACHABLE on executeBindings registration); under
  that convention the $N rows in batches 59-61 flip together to REACHABLE INFERRED.
  $N numbering is generated-class declaration order, NOT bytecode-verified.
- No row is flipped to UNREACHABLE: absence from the printed subset is not evidence,
  and no saved full-repo grep exists for these generated names (rule F/C5 class-name
  grep required before any UNREACHABLE claim).

## Why every row is OPEN (not UNREACHABLE proposed, not REACHABLE proposed)

1. **Generated source is absent from the upload.** Every row is a generated `*Binding` static (`bind`/`inflate`). The generated `*Binding`, `*BindingImpl`, and `databinding.DataBinderMapperImpl` sources are not in the tree, so no definition and no generated dispatch step can be printed from them in this pass.
2. **Direct-call absence does not close a generated row.** The freshly fetched saved grep proves **no direct app-code call** to any batch 5–7 `bind`/`inflate` static in the app Java tree, and its positive controls prove the grep was not empty (DataBindingUtil.inflate in 30 files; setContentView in the three activities above). That proves app code goes through `DataBindingUtil`. It does NOT prove generated code never calls these statics, and a grep over app source cannot rule out generated dispatch.
3. **The flip to UNREACHABLE rests on an unprinted premise.** It requires the premise that generated mapper code constructs binding objects directly (via constructors / `getDataBinder`) and never routes through the base `bind`/`inflate` statics. That premise is not printed in the grep file, in any audit, or in either layout fetched here. The batch-10 audit applied exactly this reasoning to identical-shape rows (`ItemTorrentDownloadableFileBinding` / `ItemTorrentListBinding` / `ItemTrackersListBinding` / `TagsListItemBinding`) and left them OPEN ("DataBindingUtil request exists; generated bind dispatch absent" → OPEN). Flipping batches 5–7 while batch 10 stays OPEN on the same ground would bake in the inconsistency the batches 1–12 verification flags for merge. The merger must reconcile that inconsistency project-wide, not in these batch files.
4. **Task rule.** Never flip OPEN to UNREACHABLE without a saved full-repo grep. No saved full-repo grep was run in this pass, and the saved grep above is an app-tree grep, not a full-repo grep for generated code (generated code is absent from the tree it searched). Therefore no row is proposed UNREACHABLE, even though the plan says UNREACHABLE and the direct-call grep points that way.
5. **No row is REACHABLE either.** No printed direct call names any row's static, and no printed generated dispatch names any row's static. A `DataBindingUtil` request for the row's layout proves the layout is bound; it does not prove the base `bind`/`inflate` static runs, because the request goes through the mapper/Impl path whose dispatch is unprinted.
6. **Binding-variant gap (per row, undecided).** Even where a DataBindingUtil request for a layout is printed, nothing printed selects WHICH of the 6 overloads (bind vs inflate; with vs without the `Object` DataBindingComponent argument; with vs without parent/attachToParent) the framework or mapper would invoke, if it invoked a base Binding static at all. The 6 overloads of one class therefore cannot be distinguished on this evidence, and no single overload can be promoted while its siblings stay OPEN on the same evidence.
7. **Child bindings do not change this.** Two batch-5 classes have no direct DataBindingUtil site at all: `AddTorrentInfoBinding` is a child binding of the requested `fragment_add_torrent_info` layout (fresh include, above), and `DetailTorrentAppbarBinding` is a child binding of the requested `fragment_detail_torrent` layout (fresh include, above). Their creation, if any, is by generated parent-Impl code, which is unprinted. They are OPEN on the same ground as the directly requested classes, not on a weaker ground — and the include does not make them REACHABLE, because the parent Impl dispatch is unprinted.

## confirmed_by convention in this file

- `INFERRED` for every OPEN row, following the batch-2 / batch-5 merge-ready convention: at best an inference, because the generated source is absent. It does NOT mean the row is close to closed; it means the ceiling of what can be said without generated source or a saved full-repo grep covering generated code.
- No row uses `DYN`, `FW-MEMORY`, or `UNVERIFIED` as `confirmed_by`. No row is `SOURCE`: no definition or call site for any row's method was freshly printed in this pass (the audits and grep are printed evidence about the absence of direct calls and the presence of layout requests, not call sites for the rows themselves).
- Undecided conventions flagged per row (never decided here): **generated-mapper dispatch** (does the mapper/Impl ever call a base `bind`/`inflate` static?); **binding-variant** (which overload, if any); **strict-entry** (see below); **binding getViewModel** (no getter row in these batches — not applicable); **finalize** (no finalize row — not applicable); **$N mapping** (no anonymous/inner-class row — not applicable); **migration gate** (no Room migration row — not applicable).

## Strict-entry

OPEN rows assert no route, so no entry step is claimed for any row. Strict-entry does not change an OPEN verdict. It matters only if a row is later flipped: any future REACHABLE flip for a row in these batches would additionally need its launcher → host-activity / dialog-show / fragment-attach step printed fresh (none of those steps was freshly printed in this pass, except the two layout includes above, which are not entry steps). Do not apply a strict-entry revert to OPEN rows — they are already OPEN.

## OPEN rows and what would resolve them (15 rows, after the 2026-11-01 OPEN-closing revision)
- Rows 9, 10, 35, 36 (hasPendingBindings / invalidateAll): a printed call site, or a printed generated Impl body showing an internal caller. Fresh this pass: zero hits for hasPendingBindings, invalidateAll, executePendingBindings, or getViewModel across all 8 freshly fetched Java files (printed-subset check only, not a saved full-repo grep), so these rows stay OPEN and are NOT flipped to UNREACHABLE.
- Rows 11, 12 (base appbar onFieldChange / setVariable): a layout variant of detail_torrent_appbar that declares a <data> variable / binds an observable, or a printed generated Impl body showing a registration/variable propagation. Fresh this pass: base detail_torrent_appbar.xml (29 lines, SHA 15c7cf4f...) declares NO <data> and NO expressions, and BOTH variant appbar layouts (36 lines each, SHA be659cb6...) also declare NO <data> and NO expressions, so no observable exists for these rows on any variant.
- Rows 17, 18, 25, 26 (variant hasPendingBindings / invalidateAll): same resolver as rows 9, 10 above.
- Rows 19, 20, 27, 28 (variant onFieldChange / setVariable): same resolver as rows 11, 12 above — the freshly printed variant layouts declare no variable and no observable, so these rows cannot fire on any variant.
- Row 30 (abstract getViewModel): a printed caller (zero hits in the fresh corpus), or a merger decision adopting the executeBindings-reads-getter convention (v) (then it flips with all other binding getters).
- Rows 13-16 and 21-24 are NO LONGER OPEN (flipped in this revision, see row citations); they revert to OPEN only if the merger adopts a single fixed portrait-phone config (convention iv).
- General resolver: printed generated mapper/Impl bodies, or a saved full-repo grep in rule-F/C5 form over generated binding sources. No OPEN row may be flipped to UNREACHABLE on the basis of not being found in the printed subset.
## Caveats restated
1. Strict-entry caveat: every REACHABLE row is anchored at a printed inflate site, NOT at
   the launcher. Who shows DetailTorrentFragment / AddFeedDialog / AddTorrentInfoFragment
   was not printed in this session; under a strict launcher-entry rule all 14 REACHABLE
   rows revert to OPEN.
2. Mapper caveat: DataBinderMapperImpl selection was not printed and is not in
   final_java_only. If the merger requires a printed mapper step, all Impl rows revert
   to OPEN (the batch-17 position).
3. Row 8 flag: DetailTorrentAppbarBindingImpl.executeBindings stands on the printed
   inflate under the wave convention; if the merger requires a bound expression for
   executeBindings too, row 8 flips to OPEN with rows 11-12 (the appbar layout binds
   nothing).
4. Anonymous $N numbering and the generated 2-arg -> 3-arg ctor delegation are
   generated-code inferences, not bytecode-verified.
5. No row in this file is verified for merge: no build, sampler, grep, or C1 check was run.
   A merge step must re-derive every row from a full checkout, run the C1 checks, and
   spot-check at least 1 in 5 citations.

## Judgement rule (same as the proposal, consistent with batches 67-68)

- REACHABLE proposed, INFERRED, where a dialog prints DataBindingUtil.inflate for that
  layout AND a variable setter (setViewModel, or setDetailError for Error): the generated
  mapper returns the Impl, its constructor runs, executeBindings runs on inflate, the
  setter runs the Impl override, onFieldChange runs when a registered observable notifies,
  and onChangeViewModel* runs for the named observable. Abstract Binding <init> runs via
  the Impl super-call; abstract getters are read by generated executeBindings.
- OPEN where no printed caller/registration exists: hasPendingBindings, invalidateAll
  (no printed caller), every $N.<init>/onChange (observable mapping unprinted — Impl bodies
  absent), and DialogErrorBindingImpl.onFieldChange (detailError is a plain String argument
  at ErrorReportDialog:69, not an observable — no registration is printed, and without the
  Impl body this cannot be upgraded to a branch_never_taken claim, so it stays OPEN).
- OPEN is not UNREACHABLE: no Impl body and no full-repo grep was printed.
- Dialog-anchored caveat: the step that SHOWS each dialog was NOT printed in this pass,
  so under a strict entry-route rule every REACHABLE below reverts to OPEN. Routes are
  anchored at the dialog's onCreateDialog, not at the launcher. (A sibling agent's
  FileManagerDialog -> GoToFolderDialog route is NOT used as evidence here, as it was
  not printed in this session.)

Resolver for (v): a printed generated Impl body showing whether `executeBindings` calls the getter or reads the field directly, or a
printed `.getViewModel()` caller, or a saved full-repo grep — plus one project-wide merger decision.

### (xv) `setVariable` — rows 10 (DialogGoToFolderBindingImpl.setVariable), 24 (DialogLogFilterBindingImpl.setVariable)

Printed app calls for these two Impls: `setViewModel` only — GoToFolderDialog:100, SessionLogFilterDialog:99. Zero `.setVariable(`
hits in the 252-file corpus. Both layouts declare a `viewModel` variable and bind its observables (GoToFolder `viewModel.path`,
an ObservableField; LogFilter `viewModel.mutableParams.*`, five two-way bindings).

| Merger option | Rows 10, 24 become | Why / companion effect |
|---|---|---|
| Rule A — current batches 63/64 rule: `setVariable` needs a printed `.setVariable(` call site | OPEN (no change) | Batch 64 `setVariable` rows stay OPEN too; batch 65 `setVariable` rows 3, 16, 27, 38 would revert to OPEN |
| Rule B — batch 65 rule as written: "`setVariable` is the generated dispatch point behind `binding.setViewModel`" | REACHABLE proposed, INFERRED | MECHANICALLY BACKWARDS — in generated code `setVariable` CALLS the typed setter (`setViewModel`), not vice versa, so a printed `setViewModel` call is not evidence that `setVariable` ran. The merger may adopt Rule B only explicitly, knowing the direction is reversed; if adopted, batch 65 rows 3, 16, 27, 38 stay REACHABLE and batch 64 `setVariable` rows still do NOT flip under Rule B (no `setViewModel` call exists for those Impls — see batch 64 appendix) |
| Rule C — generic-framework-callback rule: any Impl `setVariable` is reachable via framework/generated dispatch despite zero app calls | REACHABLE proposed, INFERRED | Also flips batch 64 `setVariable` rows 4, 13, 22, 31 and batch 65 rows; this is a broader rule than Rule B and must be adopted explicitly, not smuggled in via Rule B's wording |

Batches 63, 64, and 65 MUST use ONE `setVariable` rule. This file does not pick one.
